# Measures: tests that drive a run

Machine: 14 cores, vitest 5.1.0, Node v26.13.1, one machine for every run, nothing else heavy
running. Base commit: 6d91c01bd62f5b39d2fd24ce780be4f9a3ada42b (the run's base, unchanged tree).
Date: 2026-11-22.

Reports (`reports/`): `before-1..3.tsv.gz`, three untraced full runs (`measure.mjs run`), each
22,719 tests, 22,689 passed and 30 skipped, none failed; `trace-before.tsv.gz`, the traced run
over the 58 integration files whose median time is over 10 s (130.9 s wall, all passing);
`gate-step.tsv`, the `pnpm test` step timed alone.

The tables between `<!-- BEGIN generated: … -->` and `<!-- END generated: … -->` are written by
`node spikes/tests-that-drive-a-run/measure.mjs table … --write spikes/tests-that-drive-a-run/measures.md`
from the reports under `reports/`; everything else is written by hand.

## Integration files over 10 s

Median file time of the three untraced runs; every other column from the traced run
(`reports/trace-before.tsv.gz`). Git processes are distinct trace2 sessions with a start event,
nested ones (a git started by git or by a hook) included; git s is the sum of their exit
`t_abs`. Shell cmds are the children that are not git. Records are the record writes
(`read-tree --empty`, which only the records writer issues). Repeated git is the git processes in
repeated prefixes (or repeated heads, in a `sequence` file); share is repeated git over every
git process of the file, its hooks included. Covered: over 10 s and share at least 50 %.

Check against the brief's `GIT_TRACE` counts: `heldStopRun` 2,025 git processes (brief 2,025)
and `stopRecords` 2,492 (brief 2,492), equal, so the trace shows no blind spot on these two
files. Every one of the 1,040 traced tests matched its full name in vitest's JSON report and
passed.

`acceptancePhases`, `executePlan`, `partialCommandsRun`, `phasePromptGate` and `pushedBrief`
show no git process and no shell command: they run on the fake git and shell layers
(`makeFakeGit`, stand-ins), so their time is not git's and the rule cannot cover them.
`boundedSpawn` (6 git processes, 22 children) spends its time in deliberate waits and bounds.

The covered `sequence` files call none of the four helpers; their repeated part is the head of
git commands each test shares with an earlier one. Named from reading the files' setup (median
repeated head per test, in git processes; tests repeating out of the file's tests). A later phase
declares each under the name given here. The head can overstate what is restorable where a test
writes files between identical git commands (the loss q-scope accepted), so a batch phase reads
the file before declaring the prefix.

## Outcome (2026-11-22)

The run stopped here: phase-01 was held for this verdict, and the operator chose to stop after it, abandoning the
plan and the spec. Nothing beyond these measures was changed.

- The shared prefixes are small. In `heldStopRun`, "held at phase-02" is 36 git processes of about 110 per test;
  the rest is each test's own resume, close and checks. The three slowest files are not covered: `heldStopRun`
  31.0 %, `acceptancePairReview` 6.9 %, `stopRecords` 2.4 %.
- Five covered files use the run fixtures (`heldStopClose`, `heldStopCommands`, `runList`, `stoppedRunCommands`,
  `enterPhaseStop`): at most about 110 s of the 2,021 s of integration file time, by time times share.
- The 26 `sequence` files overstate what a restore could give. Their repeated head includes the git work of the
  command each test checks (in `decideVerdicts`, the decide's own `status`, `diff` and `commit`), which no copy
  replaces; what is shared is the repository's creation, a few tenths of a second per test. Their 422 s by time
  times share is an upper bound far above what is reachable.
- Expected gain of the whole plan: about 5–10 % of the suite, 10–20 s of a 190 s gate attempt, for nine more
  phases, one of them converting 26 unlike files. Not worth it.

What this leaves: the suite's time is each test's own work, about half of it git processes that phax itself starts
while the tests drive it. A lever there is a change to how many git processes phax runs per phase, a product change
to brief on these numbers.

## Per test

One table per measured file, tests in run order, the file's hooks last. Prefix is the run-driving
helper calls the test made before its first own git or shell command; repeats says whether an
earlier test of the file drove the same parameters (a prefix with a `prepare` callback or an
injected failure never repeats).
